From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.2, F06.2, F06.80\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, thank you for coming in. Can you tell me what’s been bothering you or what brings you here today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I came in because it’s gotten to where other people can actually see that something is off with how I’m moving. Like, sometimes I feel like I’m moving underwater. I’ll be trying to answer someone or get up from a chair, and it’s like my body takes forever to do the thing. People at work notice when I take too long to respond, like I’m buffering or something.\n\nAnd then other times it’s the opposite. I’ll be at home pacing around my apartment, rubbing my hands together over and over like I’m trying to shake off a bad customer call, even when the call is already over. It’s embarrassing because it doesn’t feel subtle anymore. It’s not just in my head—people can see it. That’s mostly what made me think I should come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things that other people around you don’t seem to experience—like hearing voices, seeing things that aren’t there, or believing something strongly that others don’t share?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really see things, at least not that I can say for sure. But hearing things, yes. It’s not constant, and it’s not like a whole conversation most of the time, but it happens enough that I’ve started second-guessing myself.\n\nLike, I’ll be at home and my laptop is closed, I’m off work, there shouldn’t be any calls coming in, and I’ll hear the old headset beep—like that little sound before a customer comes through. It’s so specific that my body reacts before I even think about it, like I’m about to have to answer. I’ll look over at the laptop or check the phone, and there’s nothing. No notification, no call, nothing ringing.\n\nAnd sometimes it’s more like a voice. Usually it sounds like a supervisor kind of voice, or just that tone, saying my name. Or it’ll say something like, “too slow.” That one really bothers me because it feels like it’s commenting on me, like I’m being watched or evaluated, even when I’m alone. I know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is—it’s just… it feels real in the moment, and then afterward I’m trying to convince myself it was just in my head or some noise in the apartment.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices, do they ever talk about you in the third person, like commenting on what you’re doing or thinking? Or do they ever repeat your thoughts out loud as you’re having them?\n\nPatient: It’s more like… it comments at me, if that makes sense, not usually like two voices talking about me. I don’t really hear, like, “he’s doing this” or “he’s thinking that.” It’s not usually third person like that.\n\nThe thing I notice most is that old headset beep, even when my laptop is closed and there’s no phone ringing, no call coming in, nothing. And sometimes that supervisor-type voice will say my name, or it’ll say “too slow.” That one feels like it’s about what I’m doing, like it’s judging me in the moment, but it’s not exactly repeating my thoughts out loud. It’s more like this sharp little comment, like I’m being monitored or criticized, even if I’m just sitting there by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you believed something strongly that others around you didn’t seem to understand or agree with, even when there was no evidence to support it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there is one thing. I know it sounds kind of ridiculous when I say it out loud, and I don’t have, like, proof I can show you, but I get really stuck on this idea that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow connected to me.\n\nLike, I’ll be on job sites looking at postings or trying to apply somewhere, and if something glitches, or I don’t hear back, or the page refreshes weird, I start thinking the old system is tracking me somehow. Like it has my name attached to some kind of flag saying I’m unreliable or too slow or not worth hiring. And even when the week is otherwise okay—like I’m not having some huge crisis—I can still get really convinced of it.\n\nOther people don’t really understand when I try to explain it. They’ll say, “That’s not how that works,” or “Those systems don’t follow you around,” and part of me knows I can’t prove it. But in the moment it feels obvious, like the job sites are connected to that old software in some back-end way and I’m being marked before anyone even looks at my application. It makes it hard to tell what’s a normal rejection and what’s… I don’t know, the system already deciding something about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like certain events, like glitches on websites or delays in responses, are specifically targeted at you or have a special meaning that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually a big part of what I mean when I talk about the job sites and the old work system.\n\nLike, if I’m applying somewhere and the page freezes, or the submit button doesn’t work right away, or I don’t get a confirmation email as fast as I think I should, I don’t just think, “Oh, websites are buggy.” I mean, sometimes I can tell myself that later. But in the moment it feels like it’s happening because of me specifically. Like the ticketing software from my old job is still connected somehow and it’s following me through these other sites, flagging me as unreliable or too slow before anyone even reads my application.\n\nAnd it’s weird because it can happen even when I’m having an okay week otherwise. Like I’m not necessarily falling apart that day, but one glitch or one delayed response and I start putting it together in my head like, “Okay, there it is again, that system still has me marked.” Other people don’t seem to see it that way, and I know they’d probably say it’s just normal job search stuff, but it feels very targeted when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: How strongly do you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting things like job applications? Do you ever question that belief or is it something you feel certain about, even when others say it doesn't make sense?\n\nPatient: It depends on when you ask me, honestly. Like right now, sitting here, I can say I know it sounds… technically unlikely, or at least I know other people hear it and think I’m connecting things that don’t go together. I can say that part out loud.\n\nBut when I’m actually applying for jobs and something goes wrong on the site, it can feel really strong. Like not just a passing thought. It feels like, “No, this is the old system again, it still has me tagged somehow.” Especially if the page freezes right when I’m submitting something, or I don’t get the email back, or the application status sits there doing nothing. I start thinking it’s not random, it’s because that ticketing software from my old job is still connected to me somehow and flagging me as unreliable or too slow.\n\nAnd the frustrating part is it can happen even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. It’s not like I have to be completely overwhelmed for it to feel real. I’ll be doing pretty normal, trying to be practical, trying to apply to places, and then one of those glitches happens and I get convinced all over again.\n\nI do question it sometimes, usually after the moment passes. I’ll think, “Maybe websites just break, maybe no one is looking at this, maybe this is just job hunting being awful.” But when I’m in it, I’d say I believe it pretty strongly. Not one hundred percent every second of the day, but strong enough that it affects what I do and makes me feel like the application is already ruined before anyone even sees it.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the moment and you believe the ticketing software is still connected to you, does it feel completely and totally true to you, like there's no room for doubt, or does part of you still wonder if it might not be real?\n\nPatient: In the moment, it feels almost totally true. I don’t know if I’d say there’s literally zero room for doubt, because some part of me can still hear, like, a quieter voice saying, “This probably sounds impossible,” or “Other people would say this is just a website glitch.” But that part gets really small.\n\nWhen it’s happening, especially if I’m on a job site and something freezes or the application doesn’t go through right, my mind goes straight to, “See, it’s still connected to you.” Like the old ticketing software has my name attached to something and it’s carrying over somehow, flagging me as unreliable before I even get a chance. And even if I’ve been having an okay week—like I’m not spiraling about everything—it can still click into place really fast and feel obvious.\n\nAfterward, I can question it more. I can sit back and think, “Maybe that doesn’t make sense, maybe the site just had a problem.” But while I’m in it, it doesn’t feel like a maybe. It feels like I’m noticing something real that other people just aren’t seeing.\n\nDoctor: When you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel like a complete certainty that no one could convince you otherwise, even temporarily?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can honestly say “no one could convince me otherwise” in some permanent way, because sitting here I can kind of hear how it sounds. Like, if you or somebody I trust said, “That’s probably just the job site being broken,” I might be able to nod and say, “Yeah, okay, maybe.”\n\nBut when I’m actually in it, it gets very close to certainty. Like if I’m applying for a job and the page hangs, or the confirmation doesn’t come through, or something weird happens right at the submit part, I get convinced again that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow attached to me. Like it’s tracking me through these job sites and marking me as unreliable before anybody even looks at me.\n\nAnd what bothers me is it doesn’t only happen when I’m having a terrible day. Even if I’m having a pretty okay week, trying to be level-headed, I can still hit one of those glitches and it feels like, “There it is. That’s the system again.” In that moment, you probably could talk to me and I might not argue out loud, but inside I’d still feel like I know what’s happening. Maybe you could plant a little doubt temporarily, but it wouldn’t really let go until later.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the middle of believing that the ticketing software is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel so completely true that even if someone showed you clear evidence that it wasn't happening, you wouldn’t believe them at all?\n\nPatient: I think… if I’m being really honest, in the middle of it, clear evidence might not land the way it should. Like, if someone showed me something proving that the job site had a normal technical problem, or that the old system couldn’t possibly be connected, I might look at it and understand the words, but emotionally I’d still feel like, “No, but you’re missing the part where it’s still happening to me.”\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to be difficult. It just feels so connected in that moment. Even when I’m having an okay week, like I’m not completely falling apart or anything, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. So if an application freezes or doesn’t confirm, it feels like evidence to me, even if someone else is showing me evidence the other way.\n\nMaybe later I could think about what they showed me and question myself more. But right in the middle of it, I don’t know that I’d fully believe them. I might say, “Okay, maybe,” but inside I’d probably still feel like the software is involved somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any beliefs or thoughts that you know are unusual or hard to explain, even to yourself, such that they don’t fit with how you normally understand the world?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably what I’ve been trying to describe, just in a very specific way. It’s this belief about the ticketing software from my old job. I know, sitting here, it sounds unusual. Like, I can hear myself saying it and part of me thinks, “That doesn’t really match how computers or job sites are supposed to work.”\n\nBut even when I’m having an okay week—not totally overwhelmed or spiraling—I can still get convinced that the old ticketing system is somehow still connected to me. Like it’s tracking me through job application sites, or my name is still tied to something in it, and then it flags me as unreliable before I even get a fair chance. If an application freezes, or a confirmation email doesn’t come, or the page acts weird right when I submit something, my mind goes straight there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because normally I do understand that websites glitch and systems break and not everything is connected. I’ve worked customer service, I know software can just be bad. But in those moments it doesn’t feel like a normal glitch. It feels personal, like there’s this hidden thread from that old system following me around. And then later I can step back and think, “Why would that even be possible?” But when it’s happening, it feels really real to me.\n\nDoctor: When you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel so completely true that even if someone showed you clear evidence that it wasn’t happening, you wouldn’t believe them at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I’ve kind of said this already, but in that moment it really does feel almost completely true. Like, even if somebody showed me something that made sense logically, I don’t think it would totally break the belief while I’m in it.\n\nIt’s not always like I’m panicking or having the worst week ever, either. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain. Even when I’m having an okay week and I’m trying to be reasonable, if I’m filling out applications and something glitches, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow still tracking me through those job sites. Like it’s connected to my name somehow and flagging me as unreliable before I even get considered.\n\nSo if someone showed me evidence, I might be able to say, “Okay, I see what you’re saying,” but inside I’d probably still feel like there’s some piece they can’t see. Like the proof wouldn’t feel like it applied to what’s happening to me specifically. Later on, maybe I could question it more. But right then, yeah, it feels very real.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Yeah… I think deep down, in the middle of it, I would still feel like they were missing something. Like if a technical expert sat with me and showed me diagrams or logs or whatever and said, “See, these systems are completely disconnected,” I might understand what they’re saying on the surface. But inside, there would still be this strong feeling that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow still involved. Like it’s not just a normal connection they can see—it feels like something tied to my name, or some hidden backend thing following me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. And when an application glitches or doesn’t go through right, it feels like confirmation.

Even when I’m having an okay week, not completely upset or out of control, I can still get convinced of it. So yeah, I don’t think proof would fully settle it for me in that moment. I’d probably think, “They’re showing me what they can see, but they don’t see the part that’s affecting me.”

It feels like, “No, this is the old system again, it still has me tagged somehow.” Especially if the page freezes right when I’m submitting something, or I don’t get the email back, or the application status sits there doing nothing. I start thinking it’s not random, it’s because that ticketing software from my old job is still connected to me somehow and flagging me as unreliable or too slow.\n\nAnd the frustrating part is it can happen even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. I’ll think, “Maybe websites just break, maybe no one is looking at this, maybe this is just job hunting being awful.” But when I’m in it, I’d say I believe it pretty strongly. Like, if someone showed me something proving that the job site had a normal technical problem, or that the old system couldn’t possibly be connected, I might look at it and understand the words, but emotionally I’d still feel like, “No, but you’re missing the part where it’s still happening to me.”\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to be difficult. Even when I’m having an okay week, like I’m not completely falling apart or anything, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. So if an application freezes or doesn’t confirm, it feels like evidence to me, even if someone else is showing me evidence the other way.\n\nMaybe later I could think about what they showed me and question myself more. But right in the middle of it, I don’t know that I’d fully believe them. I might say, “Okay, maybe,” but inside I’d probably still feel like the software is involved somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any beliefs or thoughts that you know are unusual or hard to explain, even to yourself, such that they don’t fit with how you normally understand the world?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably what I’ve been trying to describe, just in a very specific way. It’s this belief about the ticketing software from my old job. I know, sitting here, it sounds unusual. Like, I can hear myself saying it and part of me thinks, “That doesn’t really match how computers or job sites are supposed to work.”\n\nBut even when I’m having an okay week—not totally overwhelmed or spiraling—I can still get convinced that the old ticketing system is somehow still connected to me. Like it’s tracking me through job application sites, or my name is still tied to something in it, and then it flags me as unreliable before I even get a fair chance. If an application freezes, or a confirmation email doesn’t come, or the page acts weird right when I submit something, my mind goes straight there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because normally I do understand that websites glitch and systems break and not everything is connected. I’ve worked customer service, I know software can just be bad. But in those moments it doesn’t feel like a normal glitch. It feels personal, like there’s this hidden thread from that old system following me around. And then later I can step back and think, “Why would that even be possible?” But when it’s happening, it feels really real to me.\n\nDoctor: When you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel so completely true that even if someone showed you clear evidence that it wasn’t happening, you wouldn’t believe them at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I’ve kind of said this already, but in that moment it really does feel almost completely true. Like, even if somebody showed me something that made sense logically, I don’t think it would totally break the belief while I’m in it.\n\nIt’s not always like I’m panicking or having the worst week ever, either. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain. Even when I’m having an okay week and I’m trying to be reasonable, if I’m filling out applications and something glitches, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow still tracking me through those job sites. Like it’s connected to my name somehow and flagging me as unreliable before I even get considered.\n\nSo if someone showed me evidence, I might be able to say, “Okay, I see what you’re saying,” but inside I’d probably still feel like there’s some piece they can’t see. Like the proof wouldn’t feel like it applied to what’s happening to me specifically. Later on, maybe I could question it more. But right then, yeah, it feels very real.\n\nDoctor: When you believe the ticketing software is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel so completely true that even if you saw direct proof—like a technical expert showing you that the systems are completely disconnected—you would still feel deep down that they were wrong and that you were right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think deep down, in the middle of it, I would still feel like they were missing something. Like if a technical expert sat with me and showed me diagrams or logs or whatever and said, “See, these systems are completely disconnected,” I might understand what they’re saying on the surface. Yeah… I know I’ve sort of answered that, but yes, when I’m really in that belief, it feels that true. Like, if some IT person or technical expert showed me proof that the old ticketing system and the job application sites aren’t connected, I think part of me would hear it and understand the explanation, but another part of me would still feel like, “No, there’s something they’re not seeing.”

So direct proof might calm me down a little on the outside, maybe, but deep down I’d probably still feel like they were wrong about my situation specifically. Like the proof applies generally, but not to whatever is happening to me.

That’s mostly what made me think I should come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things that other people around you don’t seem to experience—like hearing voices, seeing things that aren’t there, or believing something strongly that others don’t share?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t really see things, at least not that I can say for sure. But hearing things, yes. It’s not constant, and it’s not like a whole conversation most of the time, but it happens enough that I’ve started second-guessing myself.\n\nLike, I’ll be at home and my laptop is closed, I’m off work, there shouldn’t be any calls coming in, and I’ll hear the old headset beep—like that little sound before a customer comes through. It’s so specific that my body reacts before I even think about it, like I’m about to have to answer. I’ll look over at the laptop or check the phone, and there’s nothing. No notification, no call, nothing ringing.\n\nAnd sometimes it’s more like a voice. Usually it sounds like a supervisor kind of voice, or just that tone, saying my name. Or it’ll say something like, “too slow.” That one really bothers me because it feels like it’s commenting on me, like I’m being watched or evaluated, even when I’m alone. I know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is—it’s just… it feels real in the moment, and then afterward I’m trying to convince myself it was just in my head or some noise in the apartment.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices, do they ever talk about you in the third person, like commenting on what you’re doing or thinking? Or do they ever repeat your thoughts out loud as you’re having them?\n\nPatient: It’s more like… it comments at me, if that makes sense, not usually like two voices talking about me. I don’t really hear, like, “he’s doing this” or “he’s thinking that.” It’s not usually third person like that.\n\nThe thing I notice most is that old headset beep, even when my laptop is closed and there’s no phone ringing, no call coming in, nothing. And sometimes that supervisor-type voice will say my name, or it’ll say “too slow.” That one feels like it’s about what I’m doing, like it’s judging me in the moment, but it’s not exactly repeating my thoughts out loud. It’s more like this sharp little comment, like I’m being monitored or criticized, even if I’m just sitting there by myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you believed something strongly that others around you didn’t seem to understand or agree with, even when there was no evidence to support it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there is one thing. I know it sounds kind of ridiculous when I say it out loud, and I don’t have, like, proof I can show you, but I get really stuck on this idea that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow connected to me.\n\nLike, I’ll be on job sites looking at postings or trying to apply somewhere, and if something glitches, or I don’t hear back, or the page refreshes weird, I start thinking the old system is tracking me somehow. Like it has my name attached to some kind of flag saying I’m unreliable or too slow or not worth hiring. And even when the week is otherwise okay—like I’m not having some huge crisis—I can still get really convinced of it.\n\nOther people don’t really understand when I try to explain it. They’ll say, “That’s not how that works,” or “Those systems don’t follow you around,” and part of me knows I can’t prove it. But in the moment it feels obvious, like the job sites are connected to that old software in some back-end way and I’m being marked before anyone even looks at my application. It makes it hard to tell what’s a normal rejection and what’s… I don’t know, the system already deciding something about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like certain events, like glitches on websites or delays in responses, are specifically targeted at you or have a special meaning that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually a big part of what I mean when I talk about the job sites and the old work system.\n\nLike, if I’m applying somewhere and the page freezes, or the submit button doesn’t work right away, or I don’t get a confirmation email as fast as I think I should, I don’t just think, “Oh, websites are buggy.” I mean, sometimes I can tell myself that later. But in the moment it feels like it’s happening because of me specifically. Like the ticketing software from my old job is still connected somehow and it’s following me through these other sites, flagging me as unreliable or too slow before anyone even reads my application.\n\nAnd it’s weird because it can happen even when I’m having an okay week otherwise. Like I’m not necessarily falling apart that day, but one glitch or one delayed response and I start putting it together in my head like, “Okay, there it is again, that system still has me marked.” Other people don’t seem to see it that way, and I know they’d probably say it’s just normal job search stuff, but it feels very targeted when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: How strongly do you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting things like job applications? Do you ever question that belief or is it something you feel certain about, even when others say it doesn't make sense?\n\nPatient: It depends on when you ask me, honestly. Like right now, sitting here, I can say I know it sounds… technically unlikely, or at least I know other people hear it and think I’m connecting things that don’t go together. I can say that part out loud.\n\nBut when I’m actually applying for jobs and something goes wrong on the site, it can feel really strong. Like not just a passing thought. It feels like, “No, this is the old system again, it still has me tagged somehow.” Especially if the page freezes right when I’m submitting something, or I don’t get the email back, or the application status sits there doing nothing. I start thinking it’s not random, it’s because that ticketing software from my old job is still connected to me somehow and flagging me as unreliable or too slow.\n\nAnd the frustrating part is it can happen even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. It’s not like I have to be completely overwhelmed for it to feel real. I’ll be doing pretty normal, trying to be practical, trying to apply to places, and then one of those glitches happens and I get convinced all over again.\n\nI do question it sometimes, usually after the moment passes. I’ll think, “Maybe websites just break, maybe no one is looking at this, maybe this is just job hunting being awful.” But when I’m in it, I’d say I believe it pretty strongly. Not one hundred percent every second of the day, but strong enough that it affects what I do and makes me feel like the application is already ruined before anyone even sees it.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the moment and you believe the ticketing software is still connected to you, does it feel completely and totally true to you, like there's no room for doubt, or does part of you still wonder if it might not be real?\n\nPatient: In the moment, it feels almost totally true. I don’t know if I’d say there’s literally zero room for doubt, because some part of me can still hear, like, a quieter voice saying, “This probably sounds impossible,” or “Other people would say this is just a website glitch.” But that part gets really small.\n\nWhen it’s happening, especially if I’m on a job site and something freezes or the application doesn’t go through right, my mind goes straight to, “See, it’s still connected to you.” Like the old ticketing software has my name attached to something and it’s carrying over somehow, flagging me as unreliable before I even get a chance. And even if I’ve been having an okay week—like I’m not spiraling about everything—it can still click into place really fast and feel obvious.\n\nAfterward, I can question it more. I can sit back and think, “Maybe that doesn’t make sense, maybe the site just had a problem.” But while I’m in it, it doesn’t feel like a maybe. It feels like I’m noticing something real that other people just aren’t seeing.\n\nDoctor: When you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel like a complete certainty that no one could convince you otherwise, even temporarily?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can honestly say “no one could convince me otherwise” in some permanent way, because sitting here I can kind of hear how it sounds. Like, if you or somebody I trust said, “That’s probably just the job site being broken,” I might be able to nod and say, “Yeah, okay, maybe.”\n\nBut when I’m actually in it, it gets very close to certainty. Like if I’m applying for a job and the page hangs, or the confirmation doesn’t come through, or something weird happens right at the submit part, I get convinced again that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow attached to me. Like it’s tracking me through these job sites and marking me as unreliable before anybody even looks at me.\n\nAnd what bothers me is it doesn’t only happen when I’m having a terrible day. Even if I’m having a pretty okay week, trying to be level-headed, I can still hit one of those glitches and it feels like, “There it is. That’s the system again.” In that moment, you probably could talk to me and I might not argue out loud, but inside I’d still feel like I know what’s happening. Maybe you could plant a little doubt temporarily, but it wouldn’t really let go until later.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the middle of believing that the ticketing software is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel so completely true that even if someone showed you clear evidence that it wasn't happening, you wouldn’t believe them at all?\n\nPatient: I think… if I’m being really honest, in the middle of it, clear evidence might not land the way it should. Like, if someone showed me something proving that the job site had a normal technical problem, or that the old system couldn’t possibly be connected, I might look at it and understand the words, but emotionally I’d still feel like, “No, but you’re missing the part where it’s still happening to me.”\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to be difficult. It just feels so connected in that moment. Even when I’m having an okay week, like I’m not completely falling apart or anything, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. So if an application freezes or doesn’t confirm, it feels like evidence to me, even if someone else is showing me evidence the other way.\n\nMaybe later I could think about what they showed me and question myself more. But right in the middle of it, I don’t know that I’d fully believe them. I might say, “Okay, maybe,” but inside I’d probably still feel like the software is involved somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any beliefs or thoughts that you know are unusual or hard to explain, even to yourself, such that they don’t fit with how you normally understand the world?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably what I’ve been trying to describe, just in a very specific way. It’s this belief about the ticketing software from my old job. I know, sitting here, it sounds unusual. Like, I can hear myself saying it and part of me thinks, “That doesn’t really match how computers or job sites are supposed to work.”\n\nBut even when I’m having an okay week—not totally overwhelmed or spiraling—I can still get convinced that the old ticketing system is somehow still connected to me. If an application freezes, or a confirmation email doesn’t come, or the page acts weird right when I submit something, my mind goes straight there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because normally I do understand that websites glitch and systems break and not everything is connected. It feels personal, like there’s this hidden thread from that old system following me around. So yeah, I don’t think proof would fully settle it for me in that moment. I’d probably think, “They’re showing me what they can see, but they don’t see the part that’s affecting me.”\n\nDoctor: When you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you, does it feel so completely true that even if you saw direct proof—like a technical expert showing you that the systems are completely disconnected—you would still feel deep down that they were wrong and that you were right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I’ve sort of answered that, but yes, when I’m really in that belief, it feels that true. Even on a week where I’m doing mostly okay, if I’m applying to jobs and the site freezes, or something doesn’t submit right, or I don’t get an email back, I can get really convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow still tracking me through these job sites. Like my name is attached to it somewhere and it’s flagging me as unreliable before anyone even looks at my application.\n\nSo direct proof might calm me down a little on the outside, maybe, but deep down I’d probably still feel like they were wrong about my situation specifically.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I know I’ve answered it a few times, but yes, that’s basically how it feels when I’m in it. Even if someone showed me direct proof, like an expert saying, “These systems are not connected,” I don’t think it would fully land for me.

The hard part is that it doesn’t only happen when I’m having a really bad day. Even when I’m having an okay week, like I’m getting through things and trying to be practical, I can still sit down to apply for jobs and get completely convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me through those job sites. Like it recognizes my name or something attached to me, and it’s flagging me as unreliable before I even get a fair chance.

So if someone showed me proof, I might understand it in a normal, logical way, but deep down I’d probably still think they were missing the part that applies to me. It would feel like they’re wrong about what’s actually happening with my applications, even if I couldn’t explain exactly how.

Like I’m not necessarily falling apart that day, but one glitch or one delayed response and I start putting it together in my head like, “Okay, there it is again, that system still has me marked.” Other people don’t seem to see it that way, and I know they’d probably say it’s just normal job search stuff, but it feels very targeted when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: How strongly do you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting things like job applications? Do you ever question that belief or is it something you feel certain about, even when others say it doesn't make sense?\n\nPatient: It depends on when you ask me, honestly. Like right now, sitting here, I can say I know it sounds… technically unlikely, or at least I know other people hear it and think I’m connecting things that don’t go together. I can say that part out loud.\n\nBut when I’m actually applying for jobs and something goes wrong on the site, it can feel really strong. Like not just a passing thought. It feels like, “No, this is the old system again, it still has me tagged somehow.” Especially if the page freezes right when I’m submitting something, or I don’t get the email back, or the application status sits there doing nothing. I start thinking it’s not random, it’s because that ticketing software from my old job is still connected to me somehow and flagging me as unreliable or too slow.\n\nAnd the frustrating part is it can happen even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. It’s not like I have to be completely overwhelmed for it to feel real. I’ll be doing pretty normal, trying to be practical, trying to apply to places, and then one of those glitches happens and I get convinced all over again.\n\nI do question it sometimes, usually after the moment passes. I’ll think, “Maybe websites just break, maybe no one is looking at this, maybe this is just job hunting being awful.” But when I’m in it, I’d say I believe it pretty strongly. Not one hundred percent every second of the day, but strong enough that it affects what I do and makes me feel like the application is already ruined before anyone even sees it.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the moment and you believe the ticketing software is still connected to you, does it feel completely and totally true to you, like there's no room for doubt, or does part of you still wonder if it might not be real?\n\nPatient: In the moment, it feels almost totally true. I don’t know if I’d say there’s literally zero room for doubt, because some part of me can still hear, like, a quieter voice saying, “This probably sounds impossible,” or “Other people would say this is just a website glitch.” But that part gets really small.\n\nWhen it’s happening, especially if I’m on a job site and something freezes or the application doesn’t go through right, my mind goes straight to, “See, it’s still connected to you.” Like the old ticketing software has my name attached to something and it’s carrying over somehow, flagging me as unreliable before I even get a chance. And even if I’ve been having an okay week—like I’m not spiraling about everything—it can still click into place really fast and feel obvious.\n\nAfterward, I can question it more. I can sit back and think, “Maybe that doesn’t make sense, maybe the site just had a problem.” But while I’m in it, it doesn’t feel like a maybe. It feels like I’m noticing something real that other people just aren’t seeing.\n\nDoctor: When you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel like a complete certainty that no one could convince you otherwise, even temporarily?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can honestly say “no one could convince me otherwise” in some permanent way, because sitting here I can kind of hear how it sounds. Like, if you or somebody I trust said, “That’s probably just the job site being broken,” I might be able to nod and say, “Yeah, okay, maybe.”\n\nBut when I’m actually in it, it gets very close to certainty. Like if I’m applying for a job and the page hangs, or the confirmation doesn’t come through, or something weird happens right at the submit part, I get convinced again that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow attached to me. Like it’s tracking me through these job sites and marking me as unreliable before anybody even looks at me.\n\nAnd what bothers me is it doesn’t only happen when I’m having a terrible day. Even if I’m having a pretty okay week, trying to be level-headed, I can still hit one of those glitches and it feels like, “There it is. That’s the system again.” In that moment, you probably could talk to me and I might not argue out loud, but inside I’d still feel like I know what’s happening. Maybe you could plant a little doubt temporarily, but it wouldn’t really let go until later.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the middle of believing that the ticketing software is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel so completely true that even if someone showed you clear evidence that it wasn't happening, you wouldn’t believe them at all?\n\nPatient: I think… if I’m being really honest, in the middle of it, clear evidence might not land the way it should. Like, if someone showed me something proving that the job site had a normal technical problem, or that the old system couldn’t possibly be connected, I might look at it and understand the words, but emotionally I’d still feel like, “No, but you’re missing the part where it’s still happening to me.”\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to be difficult. It just feels so connected in that moment. Even when I’m having an okay week, like I’m not completely falling apart or anything, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. So if an application freezes or doesn’t confirm, it feels like evidence to me, even if someone else is showing me evidence the other way.\n\nMaybe later I could think about what they showed me and question myself more. But right in the middle of it, I don’t know that I’d fully believe them. I might say, “Okay, maybe,” but inside I’d probably still feel like the software is involved somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any beliefs or thoughts that you know are unusual or hard to explain, even to yourself, such that they don’t fit with how you normally understand the world?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably what I’ve been trying to describe, just in a very specific way. If an application freezes, or a confirmation email doesn’t come, or the page acts weird right when I submit something, my mind goes straight there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because normally I do understand that websites glitch and systems break and not everything is connected. It feels personal, like there’s this hidden thread from that old system following me around. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think when I’m really in that mindset, it does feel almost impossible to shake. I don’t want to say literally nothing could ever change my mind, because part of me knows how that sounds, and if I’m calmer I can maybe question it a little. But in the moment, when I’m applying for jobs and something weird happens on the site, it feels so obvious to me that the old ticketing software is still somehow attached to me.

Like even if someone explained it to me, or showed me evidence, or told me, “That’s not how those systems work,” I’d still have this feeling underneath that they’re not seeing the specific thing happening to me. It’s like the explanation might make sense generally, but it doesn’t touch that feeling that my name is being recognized somewhere, or that I’m being flagged as unreliable before anyone even reviews my application.

And it’s not only when I’m completely falling apart or anything. Even on an okay week, when I’m functioning and trying to be reasonable, I can still get convinced of it. So I guess… yes, in those moments, it feels very fixed. Maybe not absolutely forever, but it can feel like no explanation or opinion really gets through all the way.

Like, even if somebody showed me something that made sense logically, I don’t think it would totally break the belief while I’m in it.\n\nIt’s not always like I’m panicking or having the worst week ever, either. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain. Even when I’m having an okay week and I’m trying to be reasonable, if I’m filling out applications and something glitches, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow still tracking me through those job sites. Like it’s connected to my name somehow and flagging me as unreliable before I even get considered.\n\nSo if someone showed me evidence, I might be able to say, “Okay, I see what you’re saying,” but inside I’d probably still feel like there’s some piece they can’t see. Like if a technical expert sat with me and showed me diagrams or logs or whatever and said, “See, these systems are completely disconnected,” I might understand what they’re saying on the surface. I might even nod and say, “Okay, that makes sense.”\n\nBut inside, there would still be this strong feeling that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow still involved.   "note": "The patient's delusional belief about the ticketing software being connected to them and affecting job applications, despite acknowledging that it sounds implausible and even in the face of potential contradictory evidence, raises the possibility of Delusional Disorder (F22). The delusion appears non-bizarre, fixed, and circumscribed, not accompanied by prominent hallucinations or other symptoms of schizophrenia, which makes F22 a strong candidate. But in the moment it feels like it’s happening because of me specifically. Like the ticketing software from my old job is still connected somehow and it’s following me through these other sites, flagging me as unreliable or too slow before anyone even reads my application.\n\nAnd it’s weird because it can happen even when I’m having an okay week otherwise. Like I’m not necessarily falling apart that day, but one glitch or one delayed response and I start putting it together in my head like, “Okay, there it is again, that system still has me marked.” Other people don’t seem to see it that way, and I know they’d probably say it’s just normal job search stuff, but it feels very targeted when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: How strongly do you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting things like job applications? Do you ever question that belief or is it something you feel certain about, even when others say it doesn't make sense?\n\nPatient: It depends on when you ask me, honestly. Like right now, sitting here, I can say I know it sounds… technically unlikely, or at least I know other people hear it and think I’m connecting things that don’t go together. I can say that part out loud.\n\nBut when I’m actually applying for jobs and something goes wrong on the site, it can feel really strong. Like not just a passing thought. It feels like, “No, this is the old system again, it still has me tagged somehow.” Especially if the page freezes right when I’m submitting something, or I don’t get the email back, or the application status sits there doing nothing. I start thinking it’s not random, it’s because that ticketing software from my old job is still connected to me somehow and flagging me as unreliable or too slow.\n\nAnd the frustrating part is it can happen even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. It’s not like I have to be completely overwhelmed for it to feel real. I’ll be doing pretty normal, trying to be practical, trying to apply to places, and then one of those glitches happens and I get convinced all over again.\n\nI do question it sometimes, usually after the moment passes. I’ll think, “Maybe websites just break, maybe no one is looking at this, maybe this is just job hunting being awful.” But when I’m in it, I’d say I believe it pretty strongly. Not one hundred percent every second of the day, but strong enough that it affects what I do and makes me feel like the application is already ruined before anyone even sees it.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the moment and you believe the ticketing software is still connected to you, does it feel completely and totally true to you, like there's no room for doubt, or does part of you still wonder if it might not be real?\n\nPatient: In the moment, it feels almost totally true. I don’t know if I’d say there’s literally zero room for doubt, because some part of me can still hear, like, a quieter voice saying, “This probably sounds impossible,” or “Other people would say this is just a website glitch.” But that part gets really small.\n\nWhen it’s happening, especially if I’m on a job site and something freezes or the application doesn’t go through right, my mind goes straight to, “See, it’s still connected to you.” Like the old ticketing software has my name attached to something and it’s carrying over somehow, flagging me as unreliable before I even get a chance. And even if I’ve been having an okay week—like I’m not spiraling about everything—it can still click into place really fast and feel obvious.\n\nAfterward, I can question it more. I can sit back and think, “Maybe that doesn’t make sense, maybe the site just had a problem.” But while I’m in it, it doesn’t feel like a maybe. It feels like I’m noticing something real that other people just aren’t seeing.\n\nDoctor: When you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel like a complete certainty that no one could convince you otherwise, even temporarily?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can honestly say “no one could convince me otherwise” in some permanent way, because sitting here I can kind of hear how it sounds. Like, if you or somebody I trust said, “That’s probably just the job site being broken,” I might be able to nod and say, “Yeah, okay, maybe.”\n\nBut when I’m actually in it, it gets very close to certainty. Like if I’m applying for a job and the page hangs, or the confirmation doesn’t come through, or something weird happens right at the submit part, I get convinced again that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow attached to me. Like it’s tracking me through these job sites and marking me as unreliable before anybody even looks at me.\n\nAnd what bothers me is it doesn’t only happen when I’m having a terrible day. Even if I’m having a pretty okay week, trying to be level-headed, I can still hit one of those glitches and it feels like, “There it is. That’s the system again.” In that moment, you probably could talk to me and I might not argue out loud, but inside I’d still feel like I know what’s happening. Maybe you could plant a little doubt temporarily, but it wouldn’t really let go until later.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the middle of believing that the ticketing software is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel so completely true that even if someone showed you clear evidence that it wasn't happening, you wouldn’t believe them at all?\n\nPatient: I think… if I’m being really honest, in the middle of it, clear evidence might not land the way it should. Like, if someone showed me something proving that the job site had a normal technical problem, or that the old system couldn’t possibly be connected, I might look at it and understand the words, but emotionally I’d still feel like, “No, but you’re missing the part where it’s still happening to me.”\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to be difficult. It just feels so connected in that moment. Even when I’m having an okay week, like I’m not completely falling apart or anything, I can still get convinced that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow tracking me through job sites and flagging me as unreliable. So if an application freezes or doesn’t confirm, it feels like evidence to me, even if someone else is showing me evidence the other way.\n\nMaybe later I could think about what they showed me and question myself more. But right in the middle of it, I don’t know that I’d fully believe them. I might say, “Okay, maybe,” but inside I’d probably still feel like the software is involved somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any beliefs or thoughts that you know are unusual or hard to explain, even to yourself, such that they don’t fit with how you normally understand the world?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably what I’ve been trying to describe, just in a very specific way. It’s this belief about the ticketing software from my old job. I know, sitting here, it sounds unusual. Like, I can hear myself saying it and part of me thinks, “That doesn’t really match how computers or job sites are supposed to work.”\n\nBut even when I’m having an okay week—not totally overwhelmed or spiraling—I can still get convinced that the old ticketing system is somehow still connected to me. Like it’s tracking me through job application sites, or my name is still tied to something in it, and then it flags me as unreliable before I even get a fair chance. If an application freezes, or a confirmation email doesn’t come, or the page acts weird right when I submit something, my mind goes straight there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because normally I do understand that websites glitch and systems break and not everything is connected. I’ve worked customer service, I know software can just be bad. But in those moments it doesn’t feel like a normal glitch. It feels personal, like there’s this hidden thread from that old system following me around. And then later I can step back and think, “Why would that even be possible?” But when it’s happening, it feels really real to me.\n\nDoctor: When you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting your job applications, does it feel so completely true that even if someone showed you clear evidence that it wasn’t happening, you wouldn’t believe them at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I’ve kind of said this already, but in that moment it really does feel almost completely true. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s not only when something glitches, no. The glitches definitely set it off more strongly, like if a page freezes or an application doesn’t go through, then my mind goes straight to that. But even when I’m having an okay week and nothing specific is happening right in front of me, I still find myself thinking about it.

Like I’ll be away from the computer, or just thinking about job applications in general, and I’ll get this feeling that the ticketing software from my old job is somehow still connected to me through those job sites. Not necessarily like I’m constantly thinking about it every second, but it’s kind of sitting there in the background. And then when I apply somewhere, it feels like it’s already flagging me as unreliable before anyone even gets to look at my application.

So the website acting weird makes it feel more obvious to me, but the idea doesn’t only appear because of that. It can be there even when the week is mostly normal.

{
  "candidates": ["F22", "F20.2"],
  "note": "The patient's delusional beliefs about the ticketing software tracking them, along with auditory hallucinations (e.g., hearing the headset beep, critical voice), psychomotor disturbances (slowed movement, pacing), and lack of clear evidence for other organic or mood-related causes, point more strongly toward a psychotic disorder. I know it sounds kind of ridiculous when I say it out loud, and I don’t have, like, proof I can show you, but I get really stuck on this idea that the ticketing software from my old job is still somehow connected to me.\n\nLike, I’ll be on job sites looking at postings or trying to apply somewhere, and if something glitches, or I don’t hear back, or the page refreshes weird, I start thinking the old system is tracking me somehow. Like it has my name attached to some kind of flag saying I’m unreliable or too slow or not worth hiring. And even when the week is otherwise okay—like I’m not having some huge crisis—I can still get really convinced of it.\n\nOther people don’t really understand when I try to explain it. They’ll say, “That’s not how that works,” or “Those systems don’t follow you around,” and part of me knows I can’t prove it. But in the moment it feels obvious, like the job sites are connected to that old software in some back-end way and I’m being marked before anyone even looks at my application. It makes it hard to tell what’s a normal rejection and what’s… I don’t know, the system already deciding something about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like certain events, like glitches on websites or delays in responses, are specifically targeted at you or have a special meaning that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually a big part of what I mean when I talk about the job sites and the old work system.\n\nLike, if I’m applying somewhere and the page freezes, or the submit button doesn’t work right away, or I don’t get a confirmation email as fast as I think I should, I don’t just think, “Oh, websites are buggy.” I mean, sometimes I can tell myself that later. But in the moment it feels like it’s happening because of me specifically. Like the ticketing software from my old job is still connected somehow and it’s following me through these other sites, flagging me as unreliable or too slow before anyone even reads my application.\n\nAnd it’s weird because it can happen even when I’m having an okay week otherwise. Like I’m not necessarily falling apart that day, but one glitch or one delayed response and I start putting it together in my head like, “Okay, there it is again, that system still has me marked.” Other people don’t seem to see it that way, and I know they’d probably say it’s just normal job search stuff, but it feels very targeted when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: How strongly do you believe that the ticketing software from your old job is still connected to you and affecting things like job applications? Do you ever question that belief or is it something you feel certain about, even when others say it doesn't make sense?\n\nPatient: It depends on when you ask me, honestly. Like right now, sitting here, I can say I know it sounds… technically unlikely, or at least I know other people hear it and think I’m connecting things that don’t go together. I can say that part out loud.\n\nBut when I’m actually applying for jobs and something goes wrong on the site, it can feel really strong. Like not just a passing thought. It feels like, “No, this is the old system again, it still has me tagged somehow.” Especially if the page freezes right when I’m submitting something, or I don’t get the email back, or the application status sits there doing nothing. I start thinking it’s not random, it’s because that ticketing software from my old job is still connected to me somehow and flagging me as unreliable or too slow.\n\nAnd the frustrating part is it can happen even when I’m otherwise having an okay week. It’s not like I have to be completely overwhelmed for it to feel real. I’ll be doing pretty normal, trying to be practical, trying to apply to places, and then one of those glitches happens and I get convinced all over again.\n\nI do question it sometimes, usually after the moment passes. I’ll think, “Maybe websites just break, maybe no one is looking at this, maybe this is just job hunting being awful.” But when I’m in it, I’d say I believe it pretty strongly. Not one hundred percent every second of the day, but strong enough that it affects what I do and makes me feel like the application is already ruined before anyone even sees it.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the moment and you believe the ticketing software is still connected to you, does it feel completely and totally true to you, like there's no room for doubt, or does part of you still wonder if it might not be real?\n\nPatient: In the moment, it feels almost totally true. I don’t know if I’d say there’s literally zero room for doubt, because some part of me can still hear, like, a quieter voice saying, “This probably sounds impossible,” or “Other people would say this is just a website glitch.” But that part gets really small.\n\nWhen it’s happening, especially if I’m on a job site and something freezes or the application doesn’t go through right, my mind goes straight to, “See, it’s still connected to you.” Like the old ticketing software has my name attached to something and it’s carrying over somehow, flagging me as unreliable before I even get a chance. And even if I’ve been having an okay week—like I’m not spiraling about everything—it can still click into place really fast and feel obvious.\n\nAfterward, I can question it more. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… it doesn’t only come up when something specific happens. I mean, the glitches or the job application pages acting weird make it feel stronger, definitely, but even when I’m not actively applying, and even when the week is going kind of okay, it can still feel true in the background.

Not in a way I can explain cleanly, but it feels like it’s tracking me somehow, or recognizing me, and then flagging me as unreliable before anyone actually looks at my application.

So I guess the certainty gets louder when something happens on a website, but it isn’t only because of that. Even when nothing obvious is happening, deep down I can still feel convinced that it’s there.
